Shrimp welfare active grantmaking – outreach database

201 active entries: 69 researchers, 85 organisations, 47 industry levers / targets. Three separate /12 scales (Researcher scale; Scale A (orgs); Scale B (meta)) - scores are comparable within a scale, not across, so entries are ranked inside each Type rather than globally. Industry levers are mostly influence points, not grantees. 66 green (9+), 35 starred as possibly under-scored, 19 retired to the graveyard at the foot of the page.

Antonio Ostrensky, Ana Silvia Pedrazzani & Nathieli Cozer (GIA/UFPR)

Integrated Aquaculture Group (GIA), Federal University of Parana, Curitiba, Brazil
Research-iResearch-iiAction-i
Contact
Email (Ostrensky): ostrensky@ufpr.br
Email (Pedrazzani): anasilviap@ufpr.br

LinkedIn (Ostrensky): linkedin.com/in/antonio-ostrensky-21890a239/
Lab site: gia.org.br/portal/
Relevance
  • Built the General Welfare Index (GWI) for whiteleg shrimp: 31 measurable grow-out indicators incl. water quality, stocking density, molting, harvest/slaughter (Fishes 2024).
  • Developed non-invasive welfare-assessment methods for P. vannamei (Animals 2023).
  • Treat eyestalk ablation as a broodstock-only issue in their framework.
Read more — background

What it is — A research group inside UFPR (its own site: "grupo de pesquisas cientificas, tecnologicas e de inovacao criado e sediado na UFPR"), founded 1998 by Ostrensky with Walter Boeger. ~25 core members (five UFPR professors, four technical researchers) plus students, ~80 faces across current and alumni listings. No separate legal entity is claimed, so a grant must route through UFPR or a university foundation (UNVERIFIED which).

Delivery record — Post-GWI output is mostly elsewhere: 2024-26 indexed papers are ecotoxicology, eDNA method development and crab virology. The only shrimp-welfare follow-on found is an SSRN preprint (Jan 2026) on a GPT-based cumulative welfare assessment tool - a modelling exercise, not farm validation. A UFPR lab also published a first farmed-tilapia welfare protocol (UNVERIFIED that the lab is GIA).

Who to approach — Pedrazzani is the bridge: she has her own GIA page (anaesthesia and welfare) and authored FAI Farms' shrimp assessment protocol, so one conversation reaches both the academic and producer-facing sides. Ostrensky is founder and gatekeeper. No emails are printed on GIA's pages.

Watch-outs — Shrimp welfare is one thin strand in a broad environmental-consultancy group, and ~20 months after the GWI paper no commercial-farm validation has surfaced. The group's momentum went into eDNA and a GPT-based welfare-index tool instead.

Hook — They own the GWI but have never run it as a farm trial. Fund a time-boxed commercial-farm validation, contracted through UFPR, with Pedrazzani leading and mortality/survival as the paired endpoint.

Could work onField-validate the GWI + non-invasive markers on commercial farms and link welfare scores to survival/mortality.

No sign of departure or project pause.

Anugrah Abadi Perkasa, using a non-invasive four-domain framework - environment, nutrition, health, behaviour

Global Seafood Alliance / Best Aquaculture Practices (BAP)

World's largest whole-chain aquaculture certification; ~1,900 certified shrimp farms
Action-ii
Contact
Web: globalseafood.org/
BAP: bapcertification.org/Contact
Relevance
  • Committed (Aug 2025) to end eyestalk ablation in BAP-certified shrimp by end of 2030; 'animal health & welfare' is one of four pillars.
Read more — background

What it is — Global Seafood Alliance Inc, EIN 54-1853030, Portsmouth NH, a 501(c)(6) business league since 1999 - not a public charity, and donations are not tax-deductible, which shapes how AWF could pay it. FY2024 (filed Oct 2025): revenue USD 19.57M, expenses USD 19.17M, net assets USD 6.04M. It reports 4,308 certified producers in 45 countries at end-2025, up 3%.

Delivery record — The 2030 ablation commitment rests on GSA's own 2023 survey (published May 2024) of 100+ industry people across Thailand, Vietnam, India, China, Australia, Japan, South America and the US. Its own conclusions were that a ~10-year transition was reasonable and that 15-20 years of collaboration would be needed - so 2030 is the fast end of their internal advice. Named barriers: economics, genetic advancement, industry mindset, and lab results conflicting with commercial observation; large producers can add broodstock, small ones cannot. No shrimp stunning or water-quality welfare requirement has shipped.

Who to approach — Melanie Siggs, Director of Strategic Engagements, is the public face on welfare; Chris Weeks, Director of Program Integrity, ran the ablation survey and owns standards integrity; Mike Kocsis has been CEO since Apr 2024, with Wallace Stevens now Board Chair and Strategic Advisor (highest paid, USD 383,951).

Money — A fee-funded ~USD 20M/yr trade body; no Coefficient Giving / Open Phil or EA grant found. Additionality here is buying evidence they will not self-fund, not filling a hole.

Hook — Their own survey names the blockers, so offer to fund exactly those: ablation-free hatchery economics and genetics evidence plus a smallholder transition pathway, in exchange for pulling 2030 forward and adding stunning.

Could work onBring the ablation ban forward and add shrimp stunning/water-quality welfare criteria.

Action-i

Read more — background

Recent shrimp work

  • Still NO shrimp programme. The homepage's only shrimp reference notes that shrimps and insects are the largest farmed groups, and links out to Shrimp Welfare Project.
  • "FWI's Satellite Journey: The End of Our Remote Sensing Project (for now)" - an R&D line discontinued (year UNVERIFIED).
  • "Why We No Longer Count Stocking Density Reductions Towards Our Impact" - they tightened their own impact accounting and dropped a headline metric, which is a useful signal about rigour.
  • "Our Water Quality Ranges: Evidence and Adjustments" (Jennifer-Justine Kirsch) - active water-quality standard-setting, and the asset most directly transferable to shrimp.
  • Countries unchanged: China, India, Philippines; >5m fishes claimed.

MACRO SIGNAL (recovered 25 Jul 2026, FWI's Jan-2026 M&E post) - Their ARA programme monitored ~9.5m fishes across 179 Indian farms in 2025 at 11 fishes helped per dollar. Two numbers matter beyond FWI itself. First, 32 farms EXITED the programme in H2 2025 and 63% of those dropouts were farmers CONVERTING TO SHRIMP - because fish farming uses antibiotics and chemicals that are prohibited in shrimp exports, so traders are pushing farmers to separate cycles or leave fish altogether. Indian smallholders are actively switching into shrimp right now. Second, water-quality interventions were identified at only 7% of farm visits - which bears directly on your stated dissuader that productivity may already incentivise near-optimal conditions, making environmental harms less frequent than assumed. Worth testing before committing to Research (ii).

Could work onAdd a shrimp workstream in India's shrimp heartland where it already operates.

Centre for Aquaculture Progress (CAP)

Independent fish-welfare research think tank, Charity Entrepreneurship-incubated, Germany-based. Reviews the evidence on fish health and welfare and turns it into welfare standards and assessment protocols. Stated mission is improving aquaculture for consumers, farmers and fish.
!Noteworthy — score may understate thisScores 6 because every factor reads it as fish-in-Europe, which is exactly what it is today. But it is building the outcome-based Operational Welfare Indicator guidance that shrimp completely lacks, it is CE-incubated and small enough to shape, and porting a method costs far less than inventing one. Species and geography are the wrong lens for this entry.
Research-iiAction-ii
Contact
Email: info@centreforaquacultureprogress.org
Tel: +49 176 685 558 47
Web: centreforaquacultureprogress.org/
LinkedIn: linkedin.com/company/centre-for-aquaculture-progress
Relevance
  • Building Operational Welfare Indicator (OWI) guidance for rainbow trout, sea bream and sea bass (announced 30 Oct 2025) - the OWI method is the directly transferable asset
  • First organisation dedicated to improving fish welfare in Greece; first campaign is uptake of pre-slaughter electrical stunning for Greek sea bream and sea bass
  • Received a USD 150,000 Coefficient Giving / Open Philanthropy grant for farmed fish welfare in Europe
  • NO shrimp, prawn or crustacean work of any kind - included because the OWI methodology and the standards route are what shrimp lacks, not because of existing shrimp relevance
  • No named staff or leadership published on the site, and no legal form or founding year stated (UNVERIFIED)
Read more — background

CORRECTS THIS ENTRY - When CAP was added a few days ago I recorded "no named staff or leadership published on the site, and no legal form or founding year stated". Both are in fact published and I missed them.

What it is — NOT a German legal entity. CAP "operates through a fiscal sponsorship with Players Philanthropy Fund (Federal Tax ID 27-6601178)", a Maryland charitable trust with 501(c)(3) status, and its site terms are governed by Maryland law. Postal address is c/o UES gGmbH, Mullerstrasse 138, 13353 Berlin. Note Players Philanthropy Fund is also Animal Policy International's US fiscal sponsor.

Delivery record — Shipped: a published consumer willingness-to-pay study on electrical stunning, a stakeholder report on Mediterranean stunning practices, producer workshops, and paid consulting projects for fish producers on stunning methods. The OWI frameworks are described as "developing" - not yet delivered, which tempers the transferability argument behind the star.

Who to approach — Sean Black, Standards Specialist - previously Senior Scientific Officer for Aquaculture at the RSPCA and Marine Health Biologist at Scottish Sea Farms - who "advises on the development and standardization of welfare assessment practices across certifications". Also Naomi Murn (Research and Partnerships) and Martin Wicke (Implementation and Operations).

Watch-outs — Three staff and no open positions listed: a hard absorption ceiling, and Black is a single point of failure for the standards asset.

Hook — Buy Black's time for a penaeid OWI annex.

Could work onPort their Operational Welfare Indicator methodology to penaeid shrimp - outcome-based, method-agnostic indicators are exactly the gap in your Research (ii) bucket, and they are young enough to shape.

Seajoy / Cooke Aquaculture

Industry-frontrunner shrimp producer (Honduras/Nicaragua) with verified welfare commitments
Research-iiiAction-i
Contact
Web: seajoy.com
Relevance
  • 100% ablation-free (GSA 2016); signed SWP Humane Slaughter Pilot (2023): no ablation, electrical stunning (~400M shrimp/yr).
Read more — background

What it is — Seajoy Group, acquired November 2018 by Cooke Inc. (private, family-owned, Saint John NB). Roughly 3,800 ha of vannamei ponds in the Gulf of Fonseca across Honduras and Nicaragua, a 6,000 m2 Choluteca plant at 100,000 lb finished product/day, 13.7% of Honduran and 21.6% of Nicaraguan output, 13,000+ MT/yr, mostly to EU and UK.

Delivery record — Stunning is running, not just signed: first stunner deployed 2023, a third awaited, ~70% of volume stunned currently against 100% targeted by end-2027, on Optimar equipment (search-snippet sourced, pages not loaded - UNVERIFIED). Reported improvements are commercial: texture, fewer defects, shelf life.

Who to approach — Ismael Wong, CEO, Seajoy Group, is the quoted welfare voice (UNVERIFIED whether still in post). Bradford Price, VP Operations & Sales, owned the original ablation-free switch in 2016. Commercial lever: Labeyrie Fine Foods, the customer whose support enabled the SWP commitment, plus Lyons Seafoods and UK retail.

Money — Not a grantee. Stunners run USD 60-80k each and have been supplied free; SWP's stunner budget was described in Feb 2024 as ~EUR 2M covering 2-3 more years.

Watch-outs — Their host sector is in crisis: Honduran shrimp exports fell 67% in 2024 after the Taiwan-to-China switch, 60+ farms closed and ~6,000 jobs were lost, plus a Mexican customs closure. Welfare capex now competes with survival, and Nicaragua adds political risk.

Hook — They are already the reference case, so ask for documentation rather than change: co-published ablation-free and stunning cost and quality data that other Latin American producers and EU buyers can be shown.

Animal Sentience Committee (Defra), UK

Statutory Defra committee under the Animal Welfare (Sentience) Act 2022, whose definition of 'animal' already includes decapod crustaceans. Reports on whether ministers gave all due regard to welfare; ministers must answer Parliament within 3 months.
!Noteworthy — score may understate thisThe legal-remit half holds - decapods have been in scope since 2022, so no scope fight is needed. But the star overstated the mechanism: the committee TRIAGES its own workplan, so a submission triggers nothing by itself, and the 3-month ministerial reply attaches only to a report it chooses to write. All ten reports to date are terrestrial. Probability of selection is the bottleneck, so hand them a ready-made scrutiny candidate.
Action-ii
Contact
Email: sentiencesecretariat@defra.gov.uk
Web: gov.uk/government/groups/animal-sentience-committee
Relevance
  • Publishes reports that trigger a binding 3-month ministerial response, including on the welfare implications of differing legal definitions of 'animal' and on the Veterinary Medicines Regulations
  • The only statutory body in this database whose legal remit already covers shrimp - no scope extension is needed first
Read more — background

What it is — Six members, all terrestrial or companion-animal specialists: Michael Seals CBE (Chair), Prof Richard Bennett (Reading; council member of both the Humane Slaughter Association and UFAW), Richard Cooper (vet, ex-Defra Animal Welfare Committee), Dr Penny Hawkins, Prof Anna Meredith OBE (Keele) and Prof Christine Nicol. No aquatic or invertebrate scientist among them.

Delivery record — Ten reports to date, every one terrestrial: glue traps, lead in ammunition, XL Bullies, dog/cat/ferret imports, livestock worrying, welfare-legislation enforcement, the Renters (Reform) Bill, the Animals (Low Welfare Activities Abroad) Act, Veterinary Medicines Regulations and the Online Safety Act. Eight government responses published. ZERO output on fish, decapods or imported seafood despite decapods being in scope since 2022.

Who to approach — Secretariat sentiencesecretariat@defra.gov.uk; guidance line asc.guidance@defra.gov.uk. Richard Bennett is the plausible internal champion given his slaughter and welfare-economics background.

Watch-outs — FLAG PARTLY FAILS. The legal-remit half holds: decapods are already in scope, so no scope fight is needed. But "compels a ministerial reply" does NOT hold as an on-demand lever. The committee states it independently selects which policies to look at using a TRIAGE process, so an external submission enters a queue and triggers nothing by itself - the 3-month ministerial reply attaches only to a report the committee chooses to write. Its remit also covers UK central-government ministerial decisions only, not devolved administrations, and it judges whether welfare was considered rather than the policy itself.

Hook — Give them a ready-made, Green-Book-shaped scrutiny candidate on shrimp import policy with the evidence pack attached, so triage is cheap for them. Probability of selection is the real bottleneck.

Could work onFile a submission on UK shrimp import policy to force a ministerial reply on the record; the sentience recognition is already in law.

EURCAW-Aqua (EU Reference Centre for Animal Welfare, Aquatic Animals)

The EU's designated reference centre for aquatic animal welfare, created by Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2024/266, applying from 1 Feb 2024. A three-party consortium - University of Crete (lead), Biology Centre of the Czech Academy of Sciences, Universitat Autonoma de Barcelona - with no legal personality of its own.
!Noteworthy — score may understate thisScores 7 only because it has no legal counterparty and no delivered decapod work - but that absence IS the opportunity, and it is time-bound. The EU's own aquatic-welfare reference centre has had decapods in its stated scope since Feb 2024 and produced nothing on them, while COM(2023) 770 - the one live EU instrument that defines 'animals' to include decapods - sits in committee. No factor on this scale can express that the gap is the ask.
Action-ii
Contact
Email: info@eurcaw-aqua.eu (printed on their own page, not guessed)
Web: eurcaw-aqua.eu/
Contact: eurcaw-aqua.eu/contact-us/
Formal input channel: eurcaw-aqua.eu/questions-to-eurcaw/
Relevance
  • THE LEVER: its own site states the centre focuses on 'fish, cephalopods and decapods', glossing decapods as crabs, lobsters, crayfish, shrimp and prawns - yet 30 months after designation no decapod publication, factsheet or workshop could be found. Every output located is finfish
  • It supplies the scientific and technical support underpinning enforcement of EU aquatic welfare rules under Regulation (EU) 2017/625, so its work programme propagates to member-state enforcement
  • Its Reflection Board carries a named 'decapods supply chain' seat, and the seat appears unfilled
  • CAVEAT: the decapod scope is a consortium self-description. It does NOT appear in the text of Implementing Decision (EU) 2024/266 as loaded, nor on the Commission's own EURCAW page - both speak only of 'aquatic animals'. Read the annexed work programme before relying on it
  • CAVEAT: no legal counterparty. A grant would have to route to Crete, the Czech Academy or UAB individually, and Commission funding may constrain external money. Both UNVERIFIED.
Could work onGet decapods onto the work programme of the EU body that already claims them in scope - the cheapest available route into member-state welfare enforcement.
Score breakdown
Shrimp/aquatic2/3Own site puts decapods including shrimp and prawns in scope, but the designating Decision says only 'aquatic animals' and every output found is finfish
Welfare+impact3/3Aquatic animal welfare is its entire designated purpose under Reg. (EU) 2017/625
Region1/3Crete, Czechia and Barcelona consortium (Europe)
Leverage & reach2/2The EU's own reference centre - its work programme feeds welfare enforcement across all member states

Walmart

World's largest retailer; BAP-certified shrimp requirement (no welfare policy yet)
!Noteworthy — score may understate thisScores 6 because Scale A reads it as a retailer with no welfare mission. The lever is far narrower and more concrete than that: their Animal Welfare Position covers six terrestrial species and addresses 'fresh and frozen meat, deli, dairy and egg suppliers', while the sibling Antibiotics position ON THE SAME PAGE says 'meat, SEAFOOD, deli, dairy and egg suppliers'. Seafood is carved out of welfare specifically, and they have already written the inclusive wording elsewhere. The ask is one edit.
Action-ii
Contact
Web: corporate.walmart.com
Relevance
  • Requires BAP-certified shrimp; partnered with Omarsa/Skretting - sustainability, not yet welfare.
Read more — background

Delivery record — Their Animal Welfare Position covers exactly six species - broiler chickens, turkeys, pork, beef cattle, dairy cattle and laying hens - each mapped to a US industry standard programme. No aquatic species appear anywhere in it. Walmart endorses both the Five Freedoms and the Five Domains as aspirations, and requires covered suppliers to publish a corporate animal welfare policy and report annually.

THE PRECISE LEVER - This is a scope line, not a missing policy. The welfare position addresses "fresh and frozen meat, deli, dairy and egg suppliers", whereas the sibling Antibiotics in Farm Animals position ON THE SAME PAGE addresses "meat, SEAFOOD, deli, dairy and egg suppliers". Seafood is carved out of welfare specifically, and they have already written the wording that includes it elsewhere.

Who to approach — No named contact is published: NOT FOUND.

Watch-outs — Both documents bind Walmart U.S. and Sam's Club U.S. only, not the international business. The Seafood Policy is a separate document last updated 14 June 2023 covering MSC/BAP/GSSI sustainability only, and the word "shrimp" does not appear in it. Two documents with two owners, so any ask must name which one it targets.

Hook — FLAG HOLDS and the lever is far narrower than "add welfare criteria": ask for ONE EDIT - add "seafood" to the Animal Welfare Position's supplier scope, matching their own antibiotics wording.

Could work onVery-high-leverage pressure target to add shrimp welfare sourcing criteria.

5

Aquaculture Action Alliance (AAA, ex-Aquaculture Working Group)

Pre-competitive supply-chain coalition on aquaculture's environmental impacts. Initiated 2021 under IDH coordination; secretariat transitioned to SFP and ASC in 2026. Members include Cargill, Skretting, BioMar, Thai Union, Seafresh/Sea Farms, Aqua Star, Regal Springs, Grupo Almar, Entobel, FollowFood and The Fishin' Co, plus ASC, SFP, the Global Salmon Initiative, Sustainable Shrimp Partnership and The Nature Conservancy.
Action-ii
Contact
Web: sustainablefish.org/industry-leadership/seafood-supplie...
SFP aquaculture roundtable route: dave.martin@sustainablefish.org (printed on SFP's roundtables index)
No named AAA chair or secretariat contact is published
Relevance
  • This is the live vehicle that absorbed SFP's Asian Farmed Shrimp Supply Chain Roundtable when that ended in March 2026 - four of the nine roundtable companies were already AAA members and most of the rest planned to join, so the buyer coalition that was the shrimp roundtable now sits here
  • Its 2026 workplan runs three working groups: carbon-footprint calculation, priorities-action coordination, and entity development into an independent association
  • Scope statements name tilapia and shrimp farming systems
  • Several members are already separate entries in this database (BioMar, Thai Union), so it is a route to them collectively rather than one at a time.
Read more — background

Still live? — Yes, and mid-reorganisation: the secretariat passed to SFP and ASC in 2026 and one of its three 2026 working groups is "entity development as an independent association". Most recent date on the page is July 2026. NOTE THE NAME: SFP's shrimp-roundtable page calls it the Aquaculture Working Group and links a URL that 404s; the live page is branded Aquaculture Action Alliance and still carries a leftover "Join the AWG" button, which is what ties the two names together. Search for both.

Who to approach — NOT FOUND at alliance level: no chair, secretariat or individual email is published, and the page says only "Contact SFP". The practical route is Dave Martin at SFP (dave.martin@sustainablefish.org), who fronts its aquaculture roundtables.

Shrimp reality check — Verbatim check of its own page returns no mention of animal welfare, stunning, eyestalk ablation, mortality, water quality or stocking density. Shrimp appears only as a farming system inside an environmental-impact remit. Its inherited shrimp roundtable never mentioned welfare either.

Hook — The timing is the entire case: a coalition drafting its own constitution and priority list is far cheaper to influence than a settled one. Fund a welfare workstream now, framed as mortality and water quality so it reads as continuous with the environmental remit rather than as a new cause - and it reaches Cargill, Skretting, BioMar and Thai Union in one room.

Watch-outs — It scores 5/12 because welfare is genuinely absent and the remit is environmental, so this is a positioning bet rather than a warm lead. Note also that it is a coalition, not yet a legal entity, so there may be no counterparty able to receive money until the entity-development workstream concludes.

Could work onGet welfare onto the priorities list while the alliance is still writing its own constitution as an independent association - mortality, water quality and harvest handling are the natural wedge because they sit closest to the environmental remit it already has.

Welfare+impact0/3Purpose is explicitly ENVIRONMENTAL impact - no mention of welfare, stunning, ablation, mortality, water quality or stocking density anywhere on its page
Region1/3SFP US and ASC Netherlands anchored; members operate in producer countries but the body itself has no on-site presence

European Commission - DG MARE, Unit A.2

Commission unit coordinating the Advisory Councils and EU aquaculture policy; co-owns aquatic animal welfare with DG SANTE.
!Noteworthy — score may understate thisScores 5 because Scale A rewards welfare mission, which a Commission unit has none of. But it owns the single live EU vehicle - the transport proposal now in co-decision that covers farmed aquatic animals AND import equivalence. Highest-leverage open door found.
Action-ii
Contact
Email: MARE-AC@ec.europa.eu
Web: oceans-and-fisheries.ec.europa.eu/
Relevance
  • Director-General Charlina Vitcheva's reply of 15 July 2026 confirms the targeted on-farm welfare revision will carry 'equivalent import requirements' but only for laying hens, broilers and pigs - 'The aquaculture sector is however not envisaged at this stage'
  • The same letter confirms the pending animal transport proposal 'does introduce equivalent standards for imports' and 'includes specific requirements for the transport of farmed aquatic animals'
  • THE OPEN DOOR: the transport proposal is the one live EU vehicle that covers farmed aquatic animals and import equivalence at the same time, and it is currently in co-decision
Read more — background

Delivery record — The vehicle is real and precisely locatable: COM(2023)0770 of 07/12/2023, procedure 2023/0448(COD), joint TRAN+AGRI committee, dossier CJ48/10/00305. Committee draft report PE770.070 (03/03/2025), with eight tranches of amendments tabled 15-16/04/2025.

Who to approach — A BETTER ROUTE THAN THE UNIT ITSELF: PECH (Fisheries) is a committee for opinion on the transport file, with rapporteur for opinion Isabella Lovin (Greens/EFA) appointed 10/10/2024 - that opinion is the formal aquatic-animal slot, and no PECH opinion document is yet listed. Joint rapporteurs are Tilly Metz (Greens/EFA, TRAN) and Daniel Buda (EPP, AGRI); shadow Anja Hazekamp (The Left).

Watch-outs — Stage reached is "Awaiting committee decision": no committee vote in the ~15 months since amendments were tabled, and no Council general approach on the file. "In co-decision" is accurate but the proposal is parked at first reading, so the risk is indefinite stall rather than closure. The original TRAN rapporteur (Deparnay-Grunenberg, 22/02/2024) was replaced by Metz on 19/09/2024, so the file has already lost continuity once.

Hook — FLAG HOLDS and is more actionable than written: the ask is a decapod amendment routed through the still-outstanding PECH opinion, not correspondence with DG MARE.

Could work onPressure point: get aquaculture back into the welfare revision, or failing that get decapods named in the transport proposal while it is still in co-decision.

IFC (World Bank Group)

Development-finance arm using loan conditionality to shape shrimp production
!Noteworthy — score may understate thisScores 5 for having no welfare mission - which is simply wrong. IFC has held a Good Practice Note on animal welfare since 2006, revised 2014, that already covers 'aquaculture in intensive and extensive systems' with a section on water quality, stocking density and handling, enforced through Performance Standard 6. 'Crustacean' appears zero times in it. The ask is a decapod annex at the next refresh, and NGO peer review is the established input route.
Action-ii
Contact
Web: ifc.org
Relevance
  • $45M Ecuador shrimp loan (Santa Priscila) conditioned on ASC/BAP replication - a proven conditionality lever.
Read more — background

Delivery record — THE WELFARE INSTRUMENT ALREADY EXISTS AND ALREADY COVERS SHRIMP'S SECTOR. IFC's Good Practice Note "Improving Animal Welfare in Livestock Operations" (Dec 2014, superseding 2006) applies to livestock "and aquaculture in intensive and extensive systems", carries a dedicated aquaculture section on water quality, stocking density and handling, and cites BAP shrimp hatchery standards. It "complements IFC's Performance Standards... in particular animal husbandry requirements for IFC clients as reflected in Performance Standard 6", enforced through the Environmental and Social Action Plan and supervision by an industry specialist, and states: "IFC has and will continue to decline projects where the system is incompatible with acceptable, science-based animal welfare". But the word "crustacean" appears ZERO times, and there is nothing on eyestalk ablation or slaughter.

Who to approach — The GPN names Robert John Hatton, Chief Industry Specialist, rhatton@ifc.org, as the animal-welfare contact for agribusiness investments - a 2014 document, so currency UNVERIFIED. Owning units: the ESG (CRKESG) Division with Manufacturing, Agribusiness and Services.

Watch-outs — The 2014 revision ran a public peer review including CIWF, RSPCA, World Animal Protection, FOUR PAWS and HSUS, which is both an established NGO input route and evidence that a refresh is the normal mechanism.

Hook — FLAG IS RIGHT FOR THE WRONG REASON - "no welfare mission" understates them. Ask for a decapod annex in a GPN refresh, not a new policy.

Could work onAdvocate to add welfare (ablation-free/humane slaughter) criteria to lending conditions.

World Bank - PROBLUE

Development-finance trust fund shaping where/how aquaculture capital flows
!Noteworthy — score may understate thisSame structural point as IFC: shapes where aquaculture capital flows, which pre-determines how much intensification happens. Hard, but the framework gives no credit for shaping the counterfactual.
Action-ii
Contact
Web: worldbank.org/en/programs/problue
Relevance
  • Finances aquaculture incl. 'climate-resilient shrimp'; was a founding partner of the NACA shrimp-environment consortium.
Read more — background

What it is — An umbrella multi-donor trust fund, in its fifth year at FY24 and recently extended to 2030. It has secured over USD 280 million from Australia, Canada, Denmark, France, Germany, Iceland, Ireland, Norway, Sweden, the UK and the US, allocated over USD 180 million to 100+ recipient economies, and added USD 37.8 million in FY24 across 37 economies. It claims every USD 1 of grant leverages USD 62 in World Bank financing.

Delivery record — Pillar 1 (Fisheries and Aquaculture) took 26.7% of approved Bank-executed funding in FY24, and PROBLUE launched the Global Aquabusiness Investment Guide at the end of FY24 - explicitly "a living framework for the sustainable development of aquaculture... as well as instructing on safeguards". A living framework with a safeguards section is the concrete insertion point.

Watch-outs — Shrimp appears on just two lines of the entire FY24 annual report; the fund reports in leverage and economies, not species or practices. No FY25 or FY26 PROBLUE annual report is indexed in the World Bank documents repository as of July 2026.

Hook — The tractable lever is NOT lending conditionality, as this entry assumed - it is the Aquabusiness Investment Guide. Propose welfare criteria for its next edition.

Could work onLending conditionality is a theoretically powerful (but hard) welfare lever.
